# libGLcore: the Kona driver in the O32 and 64-bit builds

The Kona driver routines of [libglcore-driver-functions.md](libglcore-driver-functions.md) and [libglcore-functions.md](libglcore-functions.md) were read in the N32 build of the 6.5.30m `libGLcore.so`. The same routines exist in the O32 build (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so`) and the 64-bit build (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so`); the IP35 builds under `usr/gfx/arch/IP35KONA` and `usr/gfx/arch/IP35KONA64` carry byte-identical code for every function (the function code hashes of the inventory agree for all 4868 and 4907 routines), so each entry here covers both. Each build is a separate compile, so a routine's bytes differ even when it does the same thing: the O32 compiler schedules and allocates differently and keeps arguments past the fourth on the stack, and the 64-bit build has 8-byte pointers, so structure offsets in the context are different.

## Routines that match the N32 routine

Each build has two tables. The first names each routine with its address and size in this build, its address and source file in the N32 build, and the role copied from the N32 entry. The second gives what was compared for the same routine: the ordered callees, the FIFO store count and up to six of the constants. Every row is **Inferred**: the comparison was made by a script over the disassembly of each build (`objdump -d`, with a call through a register named from the global-offset-table slot loaded into it, a direct or pc-relative call named from its target address, and a call whose register was loaded before a branch target in the same routine, for example in a delay slot of another block, listed as indirect), not by reading the routine whole. For each row the script found, in this build and in N32, the same ordered list of callees (`indirect` is a call through a pointer loaded from memory, `none` a routine that calls nothing), the same number of stores to the command FIFO page at process address 0x4000, and the same set of immediate constants of 16 or more outside the process-page words 4096 to 4200. That shows the same calls, tokens and stores modulo ABI. It does not show that every branch and every context offset is the same, so the role is only as good as that match. Offsets in the N32 role are handled per build. The 64-bit build has 8-byte pointers, so its context and process-page offsets differ: each offset the role quotes is replaced by the offset found at the same place in the two aligned instruction streams (the process-page words 4112, 4120, 4136, 4140, 4144 and 4148 are at 4128, 4144, 4168, 4176, 4184 and 4192 in every aligned access of 600 or more), and a sentence whose offsets cannot be matched is dropped with everything after it. In the O32 build the layout is that of N32, so an offset is kept only when the build's code uses the same value (the process-page words are the exception: the O32 code reaches them through a register holding 4136, so they are kept as N32 has them); dispatch-table and other offsets of that kind are not carried over. A routine whose role could not be carried over is not listed here.

## Routines whose code differs from the N32 routine

The routines below are in the O32 and 64-bit builds and do not fit the tables above: their instruction streams, call order or record sizes differ from the N32 routine. Each entry says how the build differs from the N32 entry in [libglcore-driver-functions.md](libglcore-driver-functions.md). Addresses and sizes are the build's own, taken from the inventory. `scripts/build_equivalence.py` lists the routines whose callees, FIFO store count and constants match; the entries here come from reading the disassembly (`objdump -d` of the stripped library, call targets named from the global-offset slots) and from side-by-side comparisons of call order, call arguments and memory displacements. An entry marked **Established** was followed through the code it describes; **Inferred** means the call sequence, constants and offsets were compared in full but each branch was not. The entries added in the second reading round are in the same two subsections, after the first twelve. They were read from side-by-side listings of the O32 or 64-bit routine and the N32 routine (the routine's own instructions, with calls resolved from the global-offset slot or the pc-relative target); the 64-bit differences were located with the aligned instruction streams of the two builds. The thirty-one `__glkonDrawArrays*` routines other than the vertex-only, normal-only and colour, edge-flag and vertex ones were not each followed instruction by instruction: their context offsets, token constants and frames were taken from the listings by script and checked against the name's array set, and each is labelled Inferred. The 64-bit `changebasis` matrices come from `scripts/changebasis_n64_matrix.py`, which interprets the routine because Unicorn does not execute its fused multiply-add instructions.

### O32 routines read in full

- `__glkonim_ListParameteriSGIX` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd949494, 60 bytes): **Established.** It keeps the integer value in the O32 stack argument area, passes its address as the array argument to `__glkonim_ListParameterivSGIX`, and returns that callee's result.
- `__glkonim_EdgeFlagPointerEXT` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd95a830, 220 bytes): **Established.** It reads the context pointer at address 0x1010, calls `__glim_EdgeFlagPointerEXT`, and either leaves the edge-array state unchanged or calls `compute_func_ptr` with column 1 before updating the context's array callback and dirty flags. The callback result is returned.
- `__glkonim_PollInstrumentsSGIX` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd964444, 200 bytes): **Established.** It reads the instrument status at 0x1010+24 and the context records at 0x1010+31588 through 31620. A begin raises 0x502 and returns 0; a nonzero status returns -1. A record whose sequence equals the poll count plus one copies its marker at record +32, clears the sequence, advances the read cursor by 40 bytes and returns 1; otherwise it returns 0.
- `__glkonim_GetInstrumentsSGIX` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd96450c, 220 bytes): **Established.** It reads the status at 0x1010+24 and the instrument cursors at 0x1010+31608 through 31620. A begin raises 0x502 and returns 0; nonzero status returns -1. Otherwise it counts complete ten-word records whose sequence numbers follow the poll count and returns the available count, updating the last-fetched cursor.
- `__glkonim_GenTexturesEXT_Slow` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd96f63c, 644 bytes): **Established.** It rejects a begin with 0x502 and an exhausted name range with 0x501, allocates 0x220-byte texture records through the context allocator, zeroes each record, assigns names, and inserts the records into the shared texture hash. It returns the generated names through the caller's array; allocation or insertion failure follows the library's error path.
- `__glkonim_ProcImage` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd964cc0, 592 bytes): **Established.** Unless bit 16 is already set in the context word at 0x1010+31632, it allocates the level image, creates and binds one temporary texture, sets 0x2801 to 0x2601, uploads through `__glkonim_TexImage`, reads the converted level with `getLevel`, restores the no-mode state, rebinds the previous texture, deletes the temporary name, sets the level's host-copy bit and clears bit 16.
- `__glkonim_TexImage3DEXT` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd9721c4, 144 bytes): **Established.** It accepts targets 0x806f and 0x8070, calls `__glSetError` with 0x500 for other targets, moves the two stack arguments required by the O32 calling convention, and forwards the request to `__glkonim_TexImage`.
- `__glkonim_TexImage1D` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd9722ec, 144 bytes): **Established.** It accepts targets 0xde0 and 0x8063, reports 0x500 through `__glSetError` otherwise, supplies the one-dimensional constant arguments and forwards the request to `__glkonim_TexImage`.
- `__glkonim_CopyTexImage2DEXT` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd9723f0, 128 bytes): **Established.** It accepts targets 0xde1 and 0x8095, reports 0x500 through `__glSetError` otherwise, and forwards the unchanged two-dimensional request to `__glkonim_CopyTexImage`.
- `__glkonim_TexSubImage2DEXT` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd973504, 148 bytes): **Established.** It accepts targets 0xde1 and 0x8095, reports 0x500 for other targets, supplies zero for the absent third offset and one for the absent third size, moves the O32 stack arguments, and calls `__glkonim_TexSubImage`.
- `__glkonim_CopyTexSubImage2DEXT` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd9736a8, 132 bytes): **Established.** It accepts targets 0xde1 and 0x8095, reports 0x500 otherwise, supplies the absent third-dimensional value as zero, and forwards to `__glkonim_CopyTexSubImage`.
- `__glkonim_CopyTexSubImage3DEXT` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd97372c, 124 bytes): **Established.** It accepts only target 0x806f, reports 0x500 otherwise, moves its stack-resident arguments into the O32 outgoing frame, and forwards the three-dimensional request to `__glkonim_CopyTexSubImage`.
- `__glkonim_GetTexImage` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd9737a8, 580 bytes): **Established.** It rejects a begin with 0x502, resolves the target with `targetIndex`, rejects an invalid target with 0x500 and an invalid level with 0x501, and prints a diagnostic when the level already has a host copy. For a texture-memory-only level it chooses the component count, calls `getLevel`, and invokes the object's rebind hook.
- `__glkonim_AreTexturesResidentEXT` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd97076c, 360 bytes): **Established.** It rejects a begin or invalid name with 0x502 or 0x501 and returns zero on those paths. For each name it tests the level mask in the texture object, writes one or zero to the caller's byte array, and returns one only when every requested texture is resident.
- `__glkonim_PrioritizeTexturesEXT` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd9708d4, 420 bytes): **Established.** It rejects a begin or invalid name with 0x502 or 0x501. For each valid object it clamps the incoming priority to 0.0 through 1.0, stores it at object +328, and calls `__glKonTexUpdatePriority` with the resulting priority class when the target index is valid.

- `__glkonim_SubdivPatchSGIX` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd98d2bc, 12988 bytes):
  **Established** for the setup, the command words and the vertex loop below; **Inferred** for the gather blocks. Same job as the N32 entry (the subdivision-patch command builder), organised differently in three places. First, the open note that this build "has no calls to `CopyVertex`" came from the comparison script naming a call through a global-offset page plus a small offset as the page alone: the routine calls the local `CopyVertex` four times (the call at 0xd98fad8 loads slot -32728 and adds -11724). Second, the setup loads the four array pointers from `a0` into registers, copies the eight byte-array pointers of `a1` and `a2` into stack words (sp+1092 down to sp+1064) and stores each array's count minus one at sp+1032 to sp+1044; the limits tested are 8, 9, 9 and 8, with error 0x501 on failure, as in N32. Any byte-array pointer that is null (and the whole of `a1` or `a2` when that is null) is replaced by a 32-byte zero buffer at sp+972; an eight-pass loop at +0x254 clears that buffer four bytes at a time and, guarded by index tests against 24 and 29, zeroes entries of two word tables at sp+876 and sp+760. Third, after token 0x1d1 (465) and the vertex-format word at context +0xa0fc (the code adds 0x8000 to the context and loads 8444), it branches on the format values 0x2a21, 0x2a23, 0x2a27 and 0x2a29 into four gather blocks (+0x41c, +0xbf4, +0x14b4, +0x1d98; +0x27a0 for any other value), each copying the strip vertices as 3, 4, 5 or 6 values into the local array at sp+176. The context words it writes match the N32 entry: the value count at +0xa100, the flag word at +0xa114 and the two masks at +0xa118 and +0xa11c (initially 0x00ffffff and 0x1fffffff in `s0` and `s1`). The command words follow N32: token 0x1d0 (464) with the flag word and both masks; when the flag word is not negative, the packed edge bytes (each byte read with `lb` and shifted into place, with a zero used where the matching bit of one of the masks is clear); token 0x1d4 (468) and a zero word; the vertices; a call to the end-of-primitive entry; token 0x1d2 (466) and a zero word. The vertex stage is the structural difference: N32 sends two vertices per pass, 12 passes, with the packed-vertex token in a register, while this build sends one vertex per pass, 24 passes, and re-tests the format word on every pass. That duplication is the reason N32 has 68 FIFO stores and this build 46. Per vertex the words are the token and three floats for 0x2a21; the token, three floats and a fourth value truncated to an integer for 0x2a23; the token and five floats for 0x2a27; the token, three floats, an integer-truncated fourth and two more floats for 0x2a29. The N32 routine compared is at 0xd989060, 15692 bytes.
- `__glkonResolveContext` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd96097c, 11156 bytes):
  **Established** for the structure and counts; **Inferred** for what each group sets, which follows the N32 entry's call order. The routine reads the context pointer from process word 4112 and keeps a second base, `s3`, equal to the context plus 176, so the fields of the N32 entry appear 176 lower: the packed enable words are at s3+1616 to s3+1636 (0x700 to 0x714), the five hint values at s3+1720 (0x768 to 0x778), the texture-coordinate generation block at s3+1812 (0x7c4). The dirty-group mask is at context +31584 (0x7b60): it is loaded first (bit 0x200 clears the accumulation buffer with four floats from s3+1472) and set to zero on the way out. Where N32 picks the enable or disable entry in the delay slot of one call, this build tests the bit and calls the library's enable entry (global slot -17572) in one branch or its disable entry (slot -17576) in the other with the same capability in `a0`: 133 call sites each, counted over the routine's slot loads. For example bit 0x4 of s3+1636 selects between enabling and disabling capability 0xdb2 (3506). It reloads the enable word from memory for every test, reloads `gp` after every call and leaves 471 delay slots as `nop`, which with the duplicated calls explains most of the 2540 extra bytes. The call order otherwise matches N32: the pipe-state calls from the accumulation clear to the draw-buffer selection, the current values, the depth and map-grid calls, the fog-function entry at the position where the N32 comparison shows an unnamed call, five hint calls with targets from 0xc50 (3152), the lights (vector parameters 0x1200 to 0x1202 and scalar ones from 0x1205, with 0x4000 plus the light index in `a0`) and the texture environment calls. Three groups write the FIFO directly, 50 stores in all, the same count as N32: token 0x119 (281) with a count of 12 per light, the count read from context +3588 (0xe04), then the 12 floats of each light record; token 0x1d6 (470) and a zero word, then the generation block from s3+1812; token 0x1ca (458), then `__glkon_datavn` with the word at s3+1572 (0x6d4) and a count of 61, with a zero word stored in the call's delay slot. The N32 routine compared is at 0xd961640, 8616 bytes.
- `changebasis` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd97af10, 15912 bytes):
  **Established** (the matrices below come from running this routine, not from reading its instructions). It converts a run of single-precision control values of order `n` into the coefficient set the N32 entry describes: output 0 is input 0 and output 1 is the last input, whatever the order. Run under an emulator on the unit vectors (`scripts/changebasis_o32_matrix.py`, MIPS32 big-endian, stride 1), it gives for every order from 2 to 15 a matrix whose entries are whole numbers to within 0.001. Order 3 is [1 0 0 / 0 0 1 / -1 2 -1] and order 4 is [1 0 0 0 / 0 0 0 1 / -2 3 0 -1 / 1 -3 3 -1], which agree with the two cases the N32 entry gives in words. Row 2 is (-(n-2), n-1, zeros, -1) and row 3 is (n-3, -(n-1), zeros, n-1, -(n-3)) for every order; the last row is the alternating binomial coefficients of n-1 (order 5: 1 -4 6 -4 1; the central entry of order 15 is 3432). The third row from the end in order 15 is 28, -294, 1365, -3640, 6006, -6006, 3003, 0, 0, -2002, 3003, -2184, 910, -210, 21. The arithmetic is the N32 arithmetic without fused multiply-add: 3978 instructions with 596 `mul.d`, 274 `add.d`, 277 `sub.d` and no `madd.d`, where N32 uses 236 `madd.d` and 195 `nmsub.d` in 1713 instructions; double constants are built with `lui` and `mtc1` (336 `mtc1`) rather than loaded from the constant pool (N32: 291 `ldc1`). Orders 1 to 15 are dispatched through a jump table (order minus one compared unsigned with 15, the table word added to `gp`); results go to a local array at sp+116 and are copied to the caller's output at the stride, four at a time and then singly. The N32 routine compared is at 0xd979e70, 6860 bytes.

  **Established.** Sends the descriptor's data as pairs: each pair is token 366 (0x16e) followed by the two words at the current position, for as many pairs as the count at descriptor +28; the data pointer is at descriptor +20. This build unrolls it four times: a first loop sends `count & 3` pairs, then a second loop sends four pairs per pass (eight words and four tokens per iteration, pointer advanced by 32 bytes) until the count reaches zero, so the 172 bytes send exactly the same words in the same order.   **Established.** Sends a range of vertices from the colour, edge-flag and vertex client arrays, one sender call per array per vertex in the order colour, edge-flag, vertex, as two runs. In three passes it stores the address of a head node (table +68, +108 and +148) into the slots at +56, +60 and +64, the node address in the node's own words at +36 and +32, and -1 in the node's word at +8. It then calls `__glKonInitARMTexCache` with the table, a limit of 16, 8 nodes per block and `stealARMTableCacheCallback` (0xd964f2c) as the eviction hook, and returns its result.
- `__glKonShareTexture` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd975ee0, 160 bytes, `kon_texture_api.s`):
  **Established.** Gives context `a0` a shared texture state. When `a1` is another context it takes that context's state from its word at +35500. When `a1` is 0 it calls the allocation hook (the word at +0 of `a0`) for 0xc2d8 bytes and clears them with `bzero`, without testing the allocation result. Then it adds 1 to the state's count at +4, sets bit 2 of the state's first word when the new count is 2 or more, and stores the state pointer at context +35500.
- `__glsplc_InvalidOperation` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd994540, 64 bytes):

When the free count at record +28 is not below `a1` (unsigned compare, branch-likely at 0xd991180) it returns 1, set in the likely branch's delay slot. Otherwise it calls `block_grow_region` with the record's word at +4 plus `a1` minus the free count as the first argument and the record as the second, and returns that result.

- `areLodsConsistent` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd96d118, 164 bytes, `kon_texture_api.s`):
  **Established.** Checks that the defined levels of an object fit together. - `DoDrawPixelsOrTexels` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd94fd94, 888 bytes, `kon_pixel.c`):
  **Established.** Sends the header of one pixel or texel draw and then the image. `a0` is the request record, `a1` and `a2` go into the header unchanged, and the original `a3` is spilled (sp+172) and later put into the header as one word. First it picks how the header travels, setting `a3` to the address of a staging record at sp+68 or to zero. The record is staged when the request's word at +180 is non-zero. Otherwise it goes direct when the word at +152 has bit 0x3000. Otherwise the context decides: bit 0x100 of the word at +1796 with the word at +1380 equal to 0x81c5 and the target (request +32) 0 or 3553 with bits 0x0c000000 of the word at +1792, or the target 3552 with bit 25 (tested by shifting the word left 6 and looking at the sign), or any case with bits 0x30000000 of +1792, leads to a test of bit 4 of the word at +31580, and the record is staged only when that bit is set (branch-likely branches at 0xd94fdcc, 0xd94fddc, 0xd94fdec, 0xd94fdfc, 0xd94fe04, 0xd94fe20, 0xd94fe30, 0xd94fe44 and 0xd94fe54; the load at 0xd94fe88 is never reached). When it is staged and bit 0x80 of the request word at +24 (a texel draw) is set, bit 4 is set in the word at +132. A texel draw also sets bit 0x8000 in +132 when bit 0x20 of the context word at +1796 is set, before the header is built. A texel header is token 372 followed by 20 words: the target (+32), `a1`, `a2`, the words at +144 and +148, the word at +20 masked with 3, the words at +180 and +184, the saved `a3`, then the words at +100, +104, ... +140 in steps of 4. A pixel header is token 58 followed by 8 words: `a1`, `a2`, +144, +148, +20 masked with 3, +180, +184 and the saved `a3`. Staged, the words are stored in the record at +0 (token), +4 (the count 20 or 8) and from +8 up; direct, the same words go to the command port in the same order with no count. Every path then calls `__glKonSendData` (local GOT entry 112, 0xd94eacc) with `a0`, `a1`, `a2` and the staging pointer (or zero) and returns its result. The N32 entry describes the same header words; this build moves the direct and staged cases into one routine and builds the staged record in a stack frame of 160 bytes.

  **Established.** The shared glPixelMap body: `a0` the map name, `a1` the size, `a2` the values, `a3` the value type (5123 unsigned short, 5125 unsigned int, 5126 float). Process word 4120 equal to 1 (a begin) raises 1282. A size of zero is replaced by a size of 1 with a local zero value, the type 5124 and no data send; a size not in 1 to 4096 raises 1281. The names 0xc70 to 0xc75 (3184 to 3189) must have a power-of-two size (1281 otherwise); 0xc76 to 0xc79 are not tested. The name picks a record and a group: 0xc70 the record at context +35536 (group 3184, class 0), 0xc71 +35560 (group 3185, class 3), 0xc72 to 0xc75 +35584 (group 3186, class 1), 0xc76 to 0xc79 +35608 (group 3190, class 2), any other name raises 1280. The map's index in its group (0 to 3) picks a size slot at record +8, +12, +16 or +20. The routine takes the largest of the four slots, counting the slot of this map as the new size, rounds it up to 256, 1024 or 4096, and doubles it for the 3186 and 3190 groups. When the record's old table size (word +0) is non-zero, differs from the new one and the name is not 0xc70 or 0xc71, it makes a stack buffer of two bytes per entry for each of the other slots, calls `PixelMapHostReadback` (0xd984c14) with the group, three buffer pointers in `a1` to `a3` and the fourth at 16(sp) to read their maps back, and then calls `EramAlloc` (0xd9843cc) with the class, the stack word sp+192 that this routine never writes, the old size, the new size and the rounded size at 16(sp). The path with no read-back calls `EramAlloc` the same way. If `EramAlloc` returns non-zero the record's table size is set to zero and the routine returns with nothing sent. Otherwise it stores the new table size at record +0 and the map size in its slot, and for type 5123, 5125 or 5126 writes token 54, 53 or 52 with the name, the size, the size minus 1 converted to a float and the rounded size, then the values, one word each (zero-extended halfwords for 5123), `size & 3` of them one at a time and the rest four at a time. The branch at 0xd984f88 that returns without a send is unreachable, since the index is always 0 to 3. The N32 entry says the power-of-two test covers every map; the O32 code tests only 0xc70 to 0xc75, and the N32 routine was not re-read for this.

- `computeActiveTarget` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd965230, 96 bytes, `kon_texture_api.s`):
  **Established.** Chooses the highest enabled texture target of a context and tells the pipe. `a0` is the context. A scan that reaches 0 stops with 0. Whichever way it stops, it stores the counter at `a0` +4, writes the token 375 and the counter to the command port, and returns the counter in `v0`. There is no call. The three tests are branch-likely (0xd965248, 0xd965264, 0xd965270) and the store to +4 sits in the delay slot of the first stop.
- `ReadTexels` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd952290, 836 bytes, `kon_pixel.c`):

The start address is the record word at +20 plus the format record's word at +8 times the record word at +168; the address actually sent is that plus the format record's word at +12 times the record word at +172. The row size is the record's width (+0) times the word at +172, or, when `a1` is non-null, its width less `a1` +64, plus 1, times that word; the height is +4, or +4 less `a1` +68, plus 1. A non-zero record word at +96 takes the command-port path. It calls `__glkon_SuspendSelectfeed` and writes token 82 followed by 18 words: the words at +32, +0, +4, +144, +148, +44, +48, +52, +56, +68, +60, +64, +72, +76, +80, +84, +88 and +92. It then calls `__glkona_wait_rdata` and `__glkon_ResumeSelectfeed`. It returns without copying when the context word at +35856 is non-zero, and otherwise calls `__glkonHostCopy` with the record, the reply address (context +31588 plus 8), the sent address, the row size, the column product at 16(sp) and the height at 20(sp). With +96 clear it builds a request vector on the stack. The first four words (sp+84 to sp+96) are the sent address, a total byte count, a row size and a pad, followed by the command 82, the count 18 and the 18 words listed above in the same order, then the word 1 at sp+228. When the record's word at +168 equals the computed row size, the total and the row size are the record's word at +160 and the pad is 0. Otherwise the row size is the computed one, the total is that times the height, and the pad is +168 less the row size, and a row size above +168 raises 1282 before anything is suspended. The routine then calls `__glkon_SuspendSelectfeed` and `__glKon_ioctl` with the saved context, 10000 and the vector. - `ReadPixels` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd951cf4, 1020 bytes, `kon_pixel.c`):
  **Established.** Reads one strip of framebuffer pixels into host memory. `a0` is the request record and `a1` an optional second record. The context comes from process word 4112 and a format record from the context word at +35948 (the same record `ReadTexels` uses). The start address is the record word at +20 plus the format record's word at +8 times the record word at +168. The span word sent to the pipe is the width (+0), except 8 when the width and the height (+4) are both 1. When bit 0x80 of the record word at +156 is set (a bitmap read), the format record's bit offset at +12 is taken modulo 8: if that or the width's low three bits is non-zero, a flag word at 272(s8) is set, the left position (+36) is reduced by the bit offset, and the span grows by it and is rounded up to the next multiple of 8 by adding the shortfall of the end; the byte row size is the span divided by 8, signed. With the flag clear the row size is the width times the record word at +172 or, when `a1` is non-null, the width less `a1` +64 plus 1 times that word, with the height reduced by `a1` +68 and increased by 1, and a column product of the format record's +12 times +172. A non-zero record word at +96 takes the command-port path: `__glkon_SuspendSelectfeed`, then token 57 with the left position (+36), the word at +40, the span, the height, the words at +144 and +148 and the start address masked with 3, then `__glkona_wait_rdata` and `__glkon_ResumeSelectfeed`. The reply is ignored when the context word at +35856 is non-zero. Otherwise a bitmap read calls `BitmapReadModifyWrite` (0xd951988) with the record, the reply address plus the bit offset shifted right by 3 plus 8, and the record word at +20; any other read calls `__glkonHostCopy` with the record, the reply address plus 8, the start address, the row size and, at 16(sp) and 20(sp), the column product and the height. With the word at +96 clear it builds a four-word vector at sp+124 (destination address, total bytes, row size, pad) and the sent command (token 57, count 7, the left position, the word at +40, the span, the height, +144, +148 and the destination masked with 3). A bitmap read first takes a stack scratch buffer of the record's word at +160 rounded up to 8; other reads use the start address plus the column product. When the record's +168 equals the computed row size the total and row size are the record's +160 and the pad is 0; otherwise the total is the row size times the height, the row size is the computed one and the pad is +168 less the row size, with 1282 raised when +168 is smaller. It calls `__glkon_SuspendSelectfeed`, `__glKon_ioctl` with the context, 10000 and the vector, `perror` with "KONA_DMAREAD" on a negative result, and `__glkon_ResumeSelectfeed`, and a flagged bitmap read ends with `BitmapReadModifyWrite` on the scratch buffer. - `__glkonResolveContext` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2dc3a00, 8636 bytes):
  **Inferred.** The call sequence, the delay-slot choice of enable or disable, and the three FIFO groups (tokens 0x119, 0x1d6 and 0x1ca, 50 stores) are the N32 ones. The context moves. The context pointer is read from process doubleword 4128 (N32 4112). The dirty-group mask is at the context plus 0x10000 minus 24896, which is 0x9ec0 (N32 0x7b60), and the routine stores zero there on the way out. Offsets found at the same places in the aligned instruction streams: the enable words 0x700 to 0x714 are at 0x808 to 0x81c, the table passed to `__glkon_datavn` is at 0x7d8 (N32 0x6d4), the block at N32 0x9b4 is at 0xac0, the light count 0xe04 is at 0xf68, the record read at N32 0x568 is at 0x668 and the one at N32 0x590 is at 0x690. The first test (bit 0x200) reads four floats from 1904 to 1916 past the context for the accumulation clear, 0x100 beyond the N32 offset 1648. The N32 routine compared is at 0xd961640, 8616 bytes.
- `__glkonCopyContext` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2dc1f30, 6852 bytes):

- `map_rtc` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2daa790, 376 bytes):
  **Established.** The 64-bit clock mapper, read in full. The calls and the test chain are those of the N32 entry at 0xd948750 (368 bytes): getpagesize; syssgi(13, the doubleword at sp+0); open of the path at 0x2ee37a0 with flag 0; mmap(0x10000, 8, 1, 0x12, fd, page-rounded physical address), where the descriptor is sign-extended by `sll a4,v0,0` at 0x2daa808 and the physical address is loaded into a5 from 24(sp) at 0x2daa818; then the mapping check, the clock-address sum and the 0x10100 check. Differences: the saved pointer is the doubleword at gp-296 (0x2f00670; N32 is a word at gp-16524), stored with `sd` at 0x2daa830 and read with `ld`. The stack slots are swapped against N32: the syssgi result is at 8(sp), the mask (getpagesize minus 1) at 16(sp), and the page-rounded physical address at 24(sp) (N32 has the mask at 24(sp) and the address at 16(sp)). Every fprintf in the routine passes a2 = 16 (0x10), where N32 and O32 pass 8. The messages are at 0x2ee37b0 (open failure, printed at the end of the routine, 0x2daa8e8), 0x2ee37d8 (mapping not at 0x10000), 0x2ee3800 (clock register not 0x10100) and the perror string at gp-168 (0x2f006f0). The open-failure test is `beq v0,a4` with a4 = -1 at 0x2daa7fc; the open-failure path does not store to the saved pointer. The already-mapped test is a plain `bne` at 0x2daa79c whose target 0x2daa890 is the shared return of 1, not a branch-likely. The mmap result is stored to the saved pointer in the delay slot of the test at 0x2daa82c, so a failed map stores -1 before perror (0x2daa8a0). The perror path then branches back to 0x2daa834 with a3 loaded from the saved pointer in its delay slot (0x2daa8b0), so the 0x10000 test follows and prints the second message. On the clock path the sum is stored to the saved pointer in the delay slot of `bne a3,a4` at 0x2daa888, before the test. Returns: 1 when the saved pointer is not -1 or the clock address equals 0x10100 (both to 0x2daa890); 0 for the open failure (0x2daa8e8, no store), the mapping mismatch (0x2daa848, saved pointer set to -1 at 0x2daa870) and the clock mismatch (0x2daa8b4, saved pointer set to -1 at 0x2daa8dc).

- `__glLockedMemoryInit` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2dacad0, 344 bytes):
  **Established.** The 64-bit setup of the locked arena in the record at a0, read in full against the N32 entry at 0xd94abd0 (328 bytes). The calls and their order are the N32 ones: `sysconf(11)`, `open` of `/dev/zero` with flag 2 (returns 0 when it fails), `getenv` of `GLKONA_RESERVE` with `atoi` of its value when set and 0x800000 when not, `mmap(0, length, 3, 0x101, fd, 0)` (on -1, `perror` and a return of 0), `close` (returns 0 when it fails), `getenv` of `GLKONA_RESERVE_LIMIT` with `strtol` of its value when set, and a return of 1. The differences are in the record and the registers. The record holds 8-byte pointers, so the sysconf result is stored as a doubleword at +24 (N32: a word at +16), the size word is at +16 (N32 +12) and the base pointer is stored as a doubleword at +0. After the close the stores are, in order, zero at +8, zero at +12, the base at +0 as a doubleword, then the zero byte at the base. N32 stores zero at +4, the base at +0, zero at +8, then the byte. The record pointer is kept in s0 (saved at 0(sp)) and the descriptor in s1 (saved at 24(sp)), where N32 keeps the record at 16(sp) and the descriptor at 8(sp). The mapping base is held at 8(sp) (N32: 0(sp)). The `getenv` result is spilled to 8(sp) before its `bne` test, a spill N32 does not make. The `strtol` result is stored to the word at gp-288 (0x2f00678); N32 stores its word at gp-16520.
- `kona_readconfig` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2dabec0, 396 bytes, `kon_context.c`):
  **Established.** Sends the context's buffer-configuration read-back request to the pipe; `a0` is the context (`s0`) and `a1` an argument record (`s1`). It calls `getenv` with the string at 0x2f006f8 ("GLDEBUG"); when that is set it first calls `fprintf` on `__iob` plus 64 with the format at 0x2ee3d38 ("rconf:lvl:%d:rgb:%d:ci:%d:isize:%d:rsize:%d:db:%d:st:%d:zb:%d:ss:%d:acc:%d:ms:%d:aux:%d:" and a newline) and twelve fields: the word at +3796, the bytes at +3716 and +3717, the words at +3748 and +3752, the bytes at +3718 and +3719, then the words at +3740, +3744, +3724, +3784 and +3792. If the word at the argument record's +0 is 3 it follows the doubleword at record +56 to an object and reads that object's word at +16, a mode selector: 0x1000 gives 1, 0x1001 gives 0, 0x1002 gives 2, 0x1003 gives 3 and any other value gives 0. The command port then gets the token 276 and that number, the context word at +3796 is set to -3, and the number is stored as a word at context +46368 (0xb520, formed with `li` and `daddu`). On every path the command port then gets the token 4 and ten words: the word at +3796; the word at +3752 if the byte at +3716 is non-zero, else 0; the word at +3748 if that byte is zero, else 0; the bytes at +3718 and +3719; and the words at +3784, +3740, +3744 and +3724, with the word at +3792 stored last in the delay slot of the return. No call other than `getenv` and `fprintf` is made, and `v0` still holds the constant 3 from the mode test at return.

- `__glkona_wait_rdata` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2dba9c0, 520 bytes):
  **Established.** Blocks until the pipe has written the first word of the reply buffer, then clears that word and returns without setting a return value. The first instruction pair reads the word at address 0x10100 and discards it. The buffer pointer is the doubleword at context +0x9ec8 (40648), where the context is the doubleword at absolute address 4128 (0x1020); the routine polls the word at buffer +0. It has three waiting phases, entered in order while that word stays zero. Phase 1 is a pure spin of at most 200000 (0x30d40) counter ticks, unrolled so each pass loads the word twice; it leaves as soon as a load is non-zero. Phase 2 (0x2dbaafc) calls `sginap` with 0 up to 200000 times, testing the word and then the call's result after each; either being non-zero ends the phase, and a non-zero word then returns while a still-zero word moves on to phase 3 (so a `sginap` interruption alone does not end the wait). Phase 3 starts by computing the end of the first 256 bytes of the buffer (buffer +256) and the stream `__iob` plus 64, both saved on the stack. Each pass calls `_sysconf` with 3, doubles the result, divides that signed value by 100 using the high half of a 64-bit multiply by the constant 0xa3d70a3d70a3d70b (the `.lit8` pool word at 0x2f00980) and a shift right by 6, and passes it to `sginap`. A non-zero `sginap` result, or a non-zero word read after it, goes to the common exit that stores zero at buffer +0 and returns. Only when the count in `s3` equals 3000 at the check (that is, after the 3001st pass) does it call `__glKon_ioctl` through a `bgezall` on the zero register at 0x2dbab40 (always taken, so an unconditional call) with the context in `a0` (loaded in its delay slot), request 10017 (0x2721) and a zero argument, then `fprintf` the message "GL:Wait Rdata timed out!  Partial dump follows:" plus a newline (string at 0x2ee3ef0) to that stream, and then `fprintf` each of the 64 words from buffer +0 up to the saved end address buffer +256 with the format "%.8x " (string at 0x2f00748), writing a newline (format at 0x2f00750) after every eighth word, when the signed remainder of the running word count by 8 is zero. The dump runs once, because the count has moved past 3000, and the wait then carries on in phase 3 without a time limit.

It returns the `__glSetError` result after a begin-flag test (absolute word 4144 equal to 1, error 1282) and after a target other than 0x8012 (error 1280). The checker at 0x2db9570 is called with the width, the height, the internal format and the record at sp+0, and a zero result returns. A request record at sp+8 gets the width at sp+8, 1 at sp+12 and sp+40, the format at sp+20, the type at sp+24 and the row pointer at sp+32; `__glKonParseInput`, `__glKonParseErrorDraw` and `__glKonParseHostImage` run in that order, the first two returning on a non-zero result. Row pass: the command port gets 273, 16, 0x40000000 and the token 245; the context takes the width at +1192, the height at +1196 and the internal format at +1136; the port then gets the first checker word, the width, the height, the words at sp+160 and sp+164, the row pointer's low two bits, the constant 1 and the words at sp+196 and sp+200. A non-zero width calls `__glKonSendData` with the record, the width, 1 and 0. Column pass: the record's width word (sp+8) is replaced by the height and its pointer (sp+32) by the column pointer, and only `__glKonParseHostImage` is called again. The port gets 245, the first checker word, the width, the height, the words at sp+160 and sp+164, the column pointer's low two bits, the constant 2 and the words at sp+196 and sp+200. A non-zero height calls `__glKonSendData` with the record, the height, 1, 0 and 2 in the fifth argument register. The routine ends by writing 273, 16 and 0. The row pass runs even when the width is zero, and the column pass runs even when the height is zero.
- `__glkonim_Bitmap` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2db5ff0, 700 bytes, `kon_pixel.c`):
  **Established.** The Kona glBitmap. The arguments are the width `a0`, height `a1`, the four floats xorig, yorig, xmove and ymove in `$f14` to `$f17` and the bitmap pointer in `a6`; the floats are spilled to the frame (xorig at s8-304, yorig -296, xmove -288, ymove -280) and the frame is addressed through `s8`. If the word at absolute address 4144 (the begin flag) is 1 it calls `__glSetError` with 1282 (0x502) and returns its result. A negative width or height calls `__glSetError` with 1281 (0x501) and returns its result. A zero width or zero height sends the token 6, five zero words, then xorig, yorig, xmove and ymove (xmove and ymove after the frame is torn down, in the last two instructions before the return), and returns without a call and without setting `v0`. Otherwise the low 32 bits of width times height decide the path. A product below 3137 takes the inline path: the width is rounded up to a multiple of 8 (`addiu` 7, `and` with -8), the bytes per row are that width divided by 8, and the stack grows by the byte total rounded up to 16. It calls `__glFillImage` (local GOT entry 1186) with the context from absolute doubleword 4128, the width, the height, format 0x1900, type 0x1a00, the bitmap pointer, the new stack buffer and 6 in the eighth register, then writes to the command port the token 5, the rounded width, the height, xorig, yorig, xorig plus xmove and yorig plus ymove, then the word count plus 1 (the byte total plus 3, divided by 4, rounded as a signed value) and, in the delay slot of the call, the byte total itself. It calls `__glkon_datavn` (local GOT entry 3294) with the stack buffer and the word count, and returns that call's result. A product of 3137 or more takes the host-image path: a record at s8-272 gets the width at +0, the height at +4, 6400 (0x1900) at +12, 6656 (0x1a00) at +16, the bitmap pointer at +24 and 65 at +32; `__glKonParseInput` is called and a non-zero result is returned; `__glKonParseHostImage` follows; the port then gets the token 6, the width, the height, the pointer's low two bits, the record words at +188 and +192, and the four floats in the order xorig, yorig, xmove (raw, not summed) and ymove (in the delay slot). `__glKonSendData` is called with the record, the width, the height and 0 (and the `bgezall` form, an unconditional call) and its result is returned. The inline path's sums and the host path's raw floats are different command forms for the same arguments; no test in this routine explains why.

- `setCurrentRaster` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2dc5bc0, 148 bytes, `kon_attrib.c`):
  **Established.** Sends the current raster position record to the pipe; `a0` is the record. The command port gets, in issue order, the token 270; the words at +152, +156, +160, +164 and +168; +56, +60, +64 and +68; +136, +140, +144 and +148; +112; and last, in the delay slot of the return, the word at +4 converted to a float. The values are loaded with `lwc1` and stored with `swc1`, so they are copied as floats without any conversion. The word at +4 is zero-extended (`dsll32` then `dsrl32`) and converted with `cvt.s.l`, so a word with its top bit set converts as a positive number up to 2^32 rather than as a negative one. There are no calls and no branches. This build's offsets differ from the N32 entry's, and the order of the groups is the same (five, four, four, one, then the converted word).
- `deformMapProlog` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2deae50, 244 bytes, `kon_instruments.c`):
  **Established.** Checks the arguments of a deformation-map request and, if they pass, forwards eight words to the command port. It returns 0 on success and 1 on any failure, and it is not given the context. The second argument `a1` must be 0x8194 (geometry deformation) or 0x8195 (texture deformation); any other value calls `__glSetError` with 1280 (0x500) and returns 1 (`li v0,1` after the call). Five further checks each call `__glSetError` with 1281 (0x501) and return 1: the fifth argument `a4` below 3; the stack argument 84 bytes above this routine's stack pointer (the caller's ninth argument, above the 80-byte frame) below 3; the stack argument at 116 bytes above it below 3; `a5`, the stack argument at 92 bytes and the stack argument at 124 bytes each not above 0; and the product of `a5`, the 92-byte argument and the 124-byte argument (two `mult` instructions, only the low word kept) of 9 or more. They run in that order: `a4`, the 84-byte argument, the 116-byte one, `a5`, the 92-byte one, the 124-byte one, then the product. Only on success are the command-port stores made, in this order: `a0`, `a1`, the floats `$f14`, `$f15`, `$f18` and `$f19`, the float 96 bytes above the stack pointer, and the float at 104 bytes in the delay slot of the return. The values tested (`a4`, `a5` and the stack arguments) are checked and never sent; `a2` and `a3` are not used at all. It makes no call on the success path.

- `DoDrawPixelsOrTexels` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2db3ba0, 792 bytes, `kon_pixel.c`):
  **Established.** Sends a draw-pixels or texel-load request to the pipe, either as a command block built on the stack or as direct command-port stores, and then calls `__glKonSendData`. `a0` is the request record (the parsed host image), `a1` and `a2` are two words passed through (position or size), and `a3` is copied to `a5` and sent as one word. The context is the doubleword at absolute address 4128. The routine first chooses between the block form and the direct form. The block form is taken when the record word at +188 is non-zero. It is also taken when that word is zero, the word at record +160 has none of bits 0x3000, and a chain of tests on the context decides so: the test passes when the context word at +2060 has bit 0x100 and the word at +1632 is 0x81c5, and the record word at +40 (the target) is 0 or 3553 with a context word at +2056 holding any of bits 0xc000000, or is 3552 with bit 25 of +2056 set; or when the word at +2056 has either of bits 0x30000000 (the tests that fail the first condition fall to the same check); and in any of these cases the context word at +40636 (0x9ebc) must have bit 2 set. Everything else uses the direct form. Block form: if record bit 0x80 of the word at +32 is clear it uses the short block, token 58 with 8 words; otherwise it ORs 4 into the record word at +140 (a store done in the delay slot of the second bit-0x80 test, which repeats the first and is never taken), ORs a further 0x8000 into it when bit 0x20 of the context word at +2060 is set, and builds a block of token 372 with 20 words. The short block's 8 words are `a1`, `a2`, the record words at +152 and +156, the word at +28 masked with 3, the word at +188, the word at +192 and `a5`. The long block's 20 words are the record word at +40, `a1`, `a2`, +152, +156, +28 masked with 3, +188, +192, `a5`, and then the eleven words +108 to +148 in ascending order (the +140 word as modified). The block is at sp+0 with the token first and the word count (8 or 20) second, and its address is passed to `__glKonSendData` in `a3`. The direct form (taken only when the +188 word is zero) writes the same tokens and word lists straight to the command port, with the +140 word ORed with 0x8000 (not 4) when context +2060 bit 0x20 is set, and passes 0 in `a3`; it stores the +188 word as the zero it holds. All four paths end in one `bgezall` call of `__glKonSendData` (0x2db2a40) with `a0`, `a1` and `a2` unchanged, and return its result.

  **Established.** Re-decides, after a 2D map or its enable changed, whether 2D evaluation runs on the host or in the geometry engine, and re-sends the maps. The context is the doubleword at absolute address 4128, kept in `s2`, and `s0` is the context plus 0x10000, so the offsets below are context offsets. The four active-map pointers are the doublewords at +47000, +47024, +47048 and +47072; any of them may be null. The routine adds up the word at +44 of every map present (a1) and takes the largest of the words at +8 and +12 of all of them (a4, with the first map's +8 floored at 0). A total of zero returns with nothing done. When the total is at most 208 (`slti` 209) and the largest value at most 8 (`slti` 9) it takes the geometry-engine path, otherwise the host path. Both paths set the byte at +47600 (0 for the geometry-engine path, 1 for the host path), store the function pointers for 2D coordinate evaluation at +47616 and 2D mesh evaluation at +47632 (`__glKonEvalCoord2DGe11` and `__glKonEvalMesh2DGe11`, or `__glKonEvalCoord2DHost` and `__glKonEvalMesh2DHost`, loaded from local GOT entries 321, 323, 318 and 319), and copy the four map pointers to +47008, +47032, +47056 and +47080 in that map order (+47000, +47024, +47048, +47072). The geometry-engine path also copies the byte at +47640 to +47641. Host path: for each map in turn it releases the buffer pointer kept at +47016, +47040, +47064 and +47088 through the context's free hook (the doubleword at context +24, called with the context and the buffer) if that pointer is non-zero, and for a present map it calls the context's allocation hook (the doubleword at +0) with the context and the map's +44 word times 4, stores the result as the new buffer pointer, and calls `__glKonHostMapData2D` with the map through a `bgezall` (an unconditional call). Geometry-engine path: it frees the four buffers (clearing each pointer), writes the token 45 and the byte at +47640 to the command port, calls `__glKonSendMapSpec2D` for the maps copied at +47008, +47080, +47032 and +47056, then `__glKonSendMapData2D` for the same four in that order, and returns. Each accepted name writes the token 443, the name and the value to the command port and then stores the value in the context. Words: 0xcf2 at +46300, 0xcf3 at +46304, 0xcf4 at +46308, 0xcf5 at +46312 (alignment), 0xd02 at +46256, 0xd03 at +46260, 0xd04 at +46264, 0xd05 at +46268 (alignment), 0x806b at +46272, 0x806c at +46276, 0x806d at +46316, 0x806e at +46320, 0x8130 at +46280, 0x8131 at +46284, 0x8132 at +46324 and 0x8133 at +46328. A rejected unpack alignment raises 1281 and then falls into the pack row-length handler, so a non-negative rejected value is still sent with the token 443, the name and the value, and stored at context +46256; a rejected pack alignment raises 1281 twice (the second time from the unpack alignment test it falls into) before the same send and store. - `ReadTexels` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2db5cc0, 808 bytes, `kon_pixel.c`):
  **Established.** The glGetTexImage reader, with a request record `a0` (kept in `s0`) and an optional second argument `a1`, a sub-rectangle record whose words at +64 and +68 are the rectangle origin. The context is the doubleword at absolute address 4128, and the pack-state record is the doubleword at context +46344 (0xb508, the address context +1384). The routine computes the start address (the record's doubleword at +24 plus the low 32 bits of the record word at +176 times the pack-state word at +8) and, from the record words at +0, +4, +176 and +180 and the sub-rectangle if one is given, the row byte count and row height. When it is non-zero (the command-port form) it calls `__glkon_SuspendSelectfeed`, writes the token 82 and then the eighteen record words +40, +0, +4, +152, +156, +52, +56, +60, +64, +76, +68, +72, +80, +84, +88, +92, +96 and +100 in that order, calls `__glkona_wait_rdata` through a `bgezall` (an unconditional call) and then `__glkon_ResumeSelectfeed`. If the context word at +46248 is non-zero it returns. Otherwise it calls `__glkonHostCopy` with the record, the reply pointer plus 8 (the doubleword at context +40648 plus 8), the start address, the row byte count, the pack skip-pixels byte offset and the height, and returns that call's result. When the word at +104 is zero (the kernel-transfer form) it builds a request vector on the stack: the doubleword at sp+0 is the start address plus the skip-pixels offset; if the record word at +176 equals the row byte count, the words at sp+8 and sp+12 are the record word +168 and the word at sp+16 is 0, and otherwise the words are the row byte count times the height, the row byte count, and the difference between the record's +176 and the row byte count, and a negative difference calls `__glSetError` with 1282 (0x502) and returns. It then calls `__glkon_SuspendSelectfeed`, copies the token 82, 18 and the same eighteen record words to sp+24 onward, sets the word at sp+152 to 1 and calls `__glKon_ioctl` with the context, request 10000 (0x2710) and the vector. A negative result calls `perror` with the string "KONA_DMAREAD" (0x2ee8120). Both outcomes call `__glkon_ResumeSelectfeed` and return its result.

Otherwise it calls the checker at 0x2db9570 with zero width, zero height, the internal format 6408 (0x1908) and a record at sp+0; a zero result returns. The separable filter's width and height, the context words at +1192 and +1196, must both be non-zero or the routine returns. A request record at sp+8 gets the width at sp+8, 1 at sp+12, the format at sp+20, the type at sp+24, the row pointer at sp+32 (doubleword) and 32 at sp+40; `__glKonParseInput` and `__glKonParseErrorRead` follow and a non-zero result from either returns. In a bitmap read the pack-state bit offset (the word at offset 12, low 3 bits) and the width decide the row length: when the offset is zero and the width is a multiple of 8, it is the width divided by 8 as a signed divide and no fix-up is needed; otherwise the width is rounded up with the offset to a multiple of 8, the record word at +44 is reduced by the offset, and a flag marks that a read-modify-write of the first and last bytes is needed. It calls `__glkon_SuspendSelectfeed`, then `__glKon_ioctl` with the context, request 10000 (0x2710) and the vector; a negative result calls `perror` with "KONA_DMAREAD" (0x2ee8120). `__glkon_ResumeSelectfeed` follows, and with the fix-up flag set `BitmapReadModifyWrite` is called with the record, the temporary buffer and the destination at +24. Otherwise it calls the hook that is the doubleword at state +32 with the state and the record, stores the node it returns at record +24, and does the same two stores to the object. The first word of the image at sp+520 is sample 64 | sample 0 << 8 | sample 64 << 16 | sample 128 << 24. It then takes the 64 floats in groups of four, runs `getS2p7` (0x2dc74d0) on the fourth, third, second and first in that order, and writes two words per group: the first is code 0 | code 1 << 10 | code 2 << 20 | code 3 << 30 and the second is code 3 shifted right by 2. Both paths then write the token 382 (0x17e) and the object word at +500 to the command port and call `__glkon_datavn` (local GOT entry 3294) with the image at sp+520 and 33 words, returning its result.

The object words used are +32 (bit 0x400000 is the bordered flag), +12 (the object's mode), +440, +508, +512 and +516 (three size exponents, each reduced by the level and clamped at 0 into `s0`, sp+416 and sp+392), +540 (the target), +552 (the dimension count), +580, +584, +612 and the descriptor table at +128, eight bytes per level. The routine calls `tileinfo` (0x2dcd180), `log2` (0x2dcef10), `subloadSimple` (0x2dcd080), `subloadClipLevel` (0x2dce010), `subloadBorderedLevel` (0x2dcd210) and `subcopyBorderedLevel` (0x2dcd4f0), all by local GOT entry 4 minus a constant, and returns no result.
  Sizes. For the clip-map targets 0x8170, 0x844d, 0x844e and 0x844f (tested at 0x2dce190 to 0x2dce1ac) the size comes from the level's descriptor, the doubleword at +128 plus 8 times the level. With the object word at +612 zero, or the level not above the word at +580, the two exponents are `log2` of the descriptor words at +12 and +16 (first and second dimension, depth zero). Otherwise the exponents are all the word at +584; the first offset grows by (2 to the +584, less descriptor +12) shifted right by 1 and the second by the same with descriptor +16, so a small level sits centred in the clip-map size. For every other target the border bit adds 1 to the first two exponents and, when the dimension count at +552 is 3, to the third (the third is otherwise 0). `tileinfo` is then called with the object and the addresses sp+48, sp+52 and sp+56. Each exponent is raised to its `tileinfo` output when it is smaller, and the amounts by which the outputs exceed the unraised exponents, floored at 0, are kept as the tile-count exponents.

The object mode at +12 splits the stack request pair at sp+64 and sp+68: mode 3 copies the two words the doubleword at +384 points to, mode 1 does the same, mode 0 stores the word at that pointer indexed by the level into slot `s7` of the pair and zero into the other slot (`s7` is 1 when the word at +440 equals the level's low bit), mode 2 takes the two words at the pointer indexed by the level with its low bit cleared (and sets `s7` to the level's low bit), mode 4 copies the object words +432 and +436 and zeroes `s7`; any other mode leaves the pair unwritten. Without the bordered flag and with the three tile-count exponents' sum zero it makes one call; with a non-zero sum it runs three nested loops (depth, then second dimension, then first dimension, the counts being 1 shifted left by each exponent) and calls `subloadSimple` per tile, the tile origin being the offset plus the loop index shifted left by that dimension's exponent, with the format word, the record word +0 and the address sp+64 on the stack. For names 3184 to 3189 (0xc70 to 0xc75) with a non-zero count, a count that is not a power of two raises 1281 at 0x2de491c (the test is count and count minus 1); names 3190 to 3193 are not tested. When that word is non-zero, differs from the new size (doubled for groups 3186 and 3190) and the name is neither 3184 nor 3185, it carves, for each of the four size words except the one the index names, a stack buffer of twice that word, rounded up to 16 bytes, and the buffer pointer for the named word is zero and calls `PixelMapHostReadback` (0x2de46a0) with the group in `a0` and the four buffer pointers in `a1` to `a4`.   **Established.** The Kona glColorTableSGI. `a0` is the target (saved at sp+224), `a1` the internal format (`s0`), `a2` the width (`s1`), `a3` the pixel format, `a4` the type and `a5` the pixel pointer. The context is the doubleword at absolute address 4128 (`a7`); the routine rebuilds its own `gp` from `t9` and keeps a 336-byte frame. Checks, in order: the begin flag (the word at absolute address 4144) equal to 1 raises 1282 (0x502); a pixel format outside 0x1903 to 0x190a and not 0x8000 raises 1280 (0x500); an internal format outside 0x1906 to 0x190a and 0x803b to 0x805b raises 1280; type 0x8032 with a format other than 0x1907 raises 1282, and types 0x8033 to 0x8036 with a format other than 0x1908 or 0x8000 raise 1282; a negative width, or a non-zero width that is not a power of two (width and width minus 1), raises 1281 (0x501); a target outside 0x80bc to 0x80d5 raises 1280. The routine has no working type-range test: the code at 0x2de3df8 to 0x2de3e08 sends every type, whether below 0x1400 (it is below 5127) or above (the first branch is taken), to the internal-format test, so the 1280 call at 0x2de4310 behind it is never reached. Each error path returns the result of `__glSetError`.
  The target is dispatched through the 26-entry table at 0x2ee8740 (the global-offset entry 34 page minus 30912, eight bytes an entry, index target minus 0x80bc). The fact sheet does not follow this jump, so it marks every block behind the dispatch as unreachable and reports their stack slots and callees as unused; the table words were read from the data, and every block named here is a table target or is reached from one. 0x80be to 0x80cf go to the 1280 error at 0x2de3f34. Four targets are real tables and four are probes. Real: 0x80d0 (context record +46024, kind 4), 0x80d1 (+46048, kind 5), 0x80d2 (+46072, kind 6) and 0x80bc (+46096, kind 7); each has a doubleword pointer eight bytes after the record start (stored with the record address at sp+232 and the pointer at sp+240), the kind is stored at sp+0, and sp+248 is 0. A width over 0x10000 (0x1000 for 0x80bc) raises 0x8031 and returns; for 0x80bc a width of 2049 to 4096 is accepted only with internal format 0x1909, 0x803f to 0x8042 or 0x8049 to 0x804d, and one of 1025 to 2048 only with 0x1909, 0x190a or 0x803f to 0x804d, anything else raising 0x8031. Probes: 0x80d3, 0x80d4, 0x80d5 and 0x80bd have record addresses context +6184, +6360, +6536 and +6712 stored at sp+240 (the path never reads them again), sp+232 zero and sp+248 one; a width over 0x10000 (0x1000 for 0x80bd), or a width that fails the 0x80bd format rules above (the same rules, 1025 to 2048 and 2049 to 4096) is cleared to 0 rather than refused.
  All accepted paths store the pixel format at sp+20, the type at sp+24, the pixel pointer at sp+32, the value 1 at sp+12 and sp+40 and the width at sp+8, then call `__glKonParseInput` (0x2db2630, global entry 111) with the address sp+8; a non-zero result is returned. A probe (sp+248 non-zero) then writes to the command port (absolute address 0x4000) the token 232, the target, the internal format, the width, the float of width plus -1.0 (the constant at gp minus 20, 0xbf800000 added), the words at sp+160 and sp+164, the word at sp+36 masked with 3, and the words at sp+196 and sp+200 (the last in the return's delay slot), and returns with no result set.
  A real table calls `__glKonParseHostImage` (0x2db27d0, global entry 112) with sp+8, stores 0 at sp+196 and sizes the pipe table from the width: widths to 256 give 256 (width 0 gives 0 and the word at sp+208), to 1024 give 1024, to 4096 give 4096 and a larger width keeps the width with a rounded size of 256. The size is doubled unless the internal format is 0x8049 to 0x804d. `EramAlloc` (0x2de3cc0) is called with the kind, the word at sp+212, the record's old size (its word at +0), the doubled size and the rounded size. A non-zero result is a refusal: the routine writes the token 232, the target, the internal format, 0, 0.0 as a float, the words at sp+160 and sp+164, the word at sp+36 masked with 3, 0 and the word at sp+200, zeroes the record word at +0 and the word at +20 of the block at sp+240, and returns. A zero result writes 273 (the pixel-mode token), 8 and 0x40000000, then 232, the target, internal format, width, width plus -1.0 as a float, the words at sp+160 and sp+164, the masked sp+36 word, the words at sp+196 and sp+200. It stores the doubled size at record +0 and the width in the word at +20 of the block at sp+240; with a non-zero width it calls `__glKonSendData` (0x2db2a40, global entry 114) with sp+8, the words at sp+8 and sp+12 and 0; it closes by writing 273, 8 and 0. No result is set on these paths.

The points are converted from double to float with `cvt.s.d` and stored in component-major planes of 4 times the u order times the v order bytes: the point at (u, v) goes at 4 times (v times the u order plus u) in each plane, read from the source at 8 times the u stride per u step and 8 times the v stride per v step, two components a pass. Otherwise a non-zero flag from the setup call (the order changed) calls `__glKonCleanUp2D` (0x21c0cd0) with the record, a byte at context + 0xb9f0 equal to 1 calls `__glKonHostMapData2D` (0x21c07b0) with it and any other value calls `__glKonSendMapData2D` (0x21c0620). Those calls are made by `bgezall zero`. The routine returns the last callee's result, or leaves `v0` as it was on the early exits.
- `__glkonim_Map2f` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2de1d80, 524 bytes; `usr/gfx/arch/IP35KONA64/libGLcore.so` has the same code, at 0x21c1d80):
  **Established.** The single-precision twin of `__glkonim_Map2d` in the 64-bit build, with the same frame, the same argument registers and stack slots (the range values arrive as floats in `$f13`, `$f14`, `$f17` and `$f18` and are passed on with plain moves), the same error call (0x502 through `__glSetError` when the word at absolute address 4144 is 1), the same `__glKonSetUpMap2` call and the same follow-up: the active-map test against the slot at context + 0xb798 plus 24 times the class, then `__glKonCleanUp2D`, `__glKonHostMapData2D` (byte at context + 0xb9f0 equal to 1) or `__glKonSendMapData2D`. The copy loop differs: it loads each component as a 32-bit float with `lwc1` and stores it unchanged, reading the source at 4 times the u stride per u step and 4 times the v stride per v step, with the component step of 4 bytes in the source, and writes the same component-major planes.
- `changeClamp` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2dca020, 612 bytes; `usr/gfx/arch/IP35KONA64/libGLcore.so` has the same code, at 0x21aa020):
  **Established.** The 64-bit build of the N32 routine of the same name: it records a wrap-mode change on one axis of a texture object. `a0` is the shared state (`s1`), `a1` the object (`s0`), `a2` the parameter and `a3` the mode. Parameter 0x2802 stores the mode in bits 0 and 1 of the object's byte at +577 and bit 12 of the word at +68; parameter 0x2803 uses bits 2 and 3 of the byte and bit 13; parameter 0x8072 uses bits 4 and 5 and bit 14. The byte field takes `a3` as it is; the word bit takes `a3` as it is except that modes 2 and 3 become 1 (so modes 1, 2 and 3 set the bit and mode 0 clears it). Any other parameter changes neither. Then, when bit 24 (0x1000000) of the object's word at +32 is set, it tests the state's word at +0 against 16 less (1 shifted left by the state word at +4 plus 1); a non-zero result calls `computeActiveTarget` (0x21a7560) with the state and re-reads the state word at +4; when the object's word at +552 equals that value it calls the object's function at +392 with the state and the object. It then clears bit 12 of the object's word at +64. When bit 22 (0x400000) of the +32 word is set it calls `guessBaseLevel` (0x21a8c60) with the object and `validateTexture` (0x21b0ed0) with the state, the object, the level that call returned, the object's word at +12 and 0, and repeats the bit 24 block (target refresh and function at +392) after it. It returns without setting `v0`.
- `__glKonEvalEnable` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2ddbfe0, 804 bytes; `usr/gfx/arch/IP35KONA64/libGLcore.so` has the same code, at 0x21bbfe0):
  **Established.** The 64-bit build of the N32 routine of the same name. `a0` is the enable enum; the context is the doubleword at absolute address 4128 (`a2`). The enum less 0xd80 is compared unsigned with 57 and indexes the 8-byte jump table at 0x22c8330; an enum outside it returns with `v0` untouched. The 1D map records are 56 bytes each from context + 0xb598, the 2D map records 56 bytes each from context + 0xb7f8, and in each record the enable byte is at +16 and the map class is the word at +0. Entries 0xd81 to 0xd8f, 0xd99 to 0xdaf and everything from 0xdb9 up go to the return. Arm by enum: 0xd80 (automatic normals) returns when the byte at context + 47640 is already set, otherwise clears the doubleword at context + 47048 (the active 2D normal map), sets the byte and calls `__glKonCleanUp2D` (0x21c0cd0). 0xd90 and 0xd91 (colour and index maps, records 0 and 1) set the record's enable byte unless it is set, and only when the context byte at +3557 equals 1 for 0xd91 and 0 for 0xd90 store the record's address in the doubleword at context + 46416 and call `__glKonCleanUp1D` (0x21c0960). 0xd92 (the normal map, record 2) sets its byte and stores the record's address at context + 46440 before `__glKonCleanUp1D`. 0xd93 to 0xd98 (records 3 to 8) set the byte, then compare the doubleword at context + 0xb538 plus 24 times the record's class with the record's address, and only when that slot is lower store the address there and call `__glKonCleanUp1D`. 0xdb0 and 0xdb1 (2D colour and index, 2D records 0 and 1) set the byte and, when the byte at +3557 matches (1 for 0xdb1), store the record at context + 47024 and call `__glKonCleanUp2D`. 0xdb2 (the 2D normal map) sets the byte and, only when the automatic-normal byte at context + 47640 is clear, stores its record at context + 47048 and calls `__glKonCleanUp2D`. 0xdb3 to 0xdb8 do the slot-if-lower step at context + 0xb798 plus 24 times the class with `__glKonCleanUp2D`. An arm whose enable byte was already set returns at once. The routine returns the clean-up routine's result on the paths that call it and leaves `v0` untouched otherwise; the clean-up calls are made by `bgezall zero`, an always-taken branch and link.
- `__glKonEvalDisable` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2ddc310, 1000 bytes; `usr/gfx/arch/IP35KONA64/libGLcore.so` has the same code, at 0x21bc310):
  **Inferred.** The fact sheet does not resolve this routine's jump table (its base is formed from a GOT-relative load), so it lists no callees and the claim checker reports the clean-up calls below as missing; the arms were read by hand from the listing and the table at 0x22c84f8 (all fifty-seven entries decoded), not from the sheet. The 64-bit build of the N32 routine of the same name, the counterpart of `__glKonEvalEnable`. `a0` is the enum, the context is the doubleword at absolute address 4128 (`a2`), and the enum less 0xd80 indexes the 57-entry jump table at 0x22c84f8 (an enum outside it returns). Entries 0xd81 to 0xd8f and 0xd99 to 0xdaf go straight to the return. The 1D map records are 56 bytes each from context + 0xb598 and the 2D ones from context + 0xb7f8; the enable byte is at +16 of a record. 0xd80 (automatic normals) returns when the byte at context + 47640 is already clear; otherwise it clears that byte, stores the address context + 0xb868 in the doubleword at context + 47048 when the byte at context + 47224 is non-zero, and runs the 2D clean-up routine at 0x21c0cd0. 0xd90 and 0xd91 return when the record's enable byte is clear; otherwise they clear it and, only when the context byte at +3557 equals 1 for 0xd91 and 0 for 0xd90, zero the doubleword at context + 46416 and call `__glKonCleanUp1D` (0x21c0960). 0xd92 returns when the byte at context + 46616 is clear, otherwise zeroes the doubleword at context + 46440, runs the 1D clean-up routine and clears the byte in the call's delay slot. 0xd93 to 0xd96 clear the record's enable byte and, only when the doubleword at context + 46464 is this record, walk down the lower records in steps of 56 bytes to context + 0xb640, taking the first whose enable byte is set (or 0), store that in the doubleword and call `__glKonCleanUp1D`. 0xd97 and 0xd98 clear the byte and, only when the doubleword at context + 46392 is this record, store context + 0xb720 there when the byte at context + 46896 is set and 0 when it is clear, then call `__glKonCleanUp1D`. 0xdb0 and 0xdb1 mirror 0xd90 and 0xd91 on the 2D records: they zero the doubleword at context + 47024 and run the 2D clean-up routine. 0xdb2 clears the byte at context + 47224 and, when the automatic-normal byte at context + 47640 is clear, zeroes the doubleword at context + 47048 and runs the 2D clean-up routine. 0xdb3 to 0xdb6 mirror 0xd93 to 0xd96 on the 2D records (the doubleword at context + 47072, the walk down to context + 0xb8a0, `__glKonCleanUp2D`). 0xdb7 and 0xdb8 mirror 0xd97 and 0xd98 (the doubleword at context + 47000, the fallback record at context + 0xb980 when the byte at context + 47504 is set, otherwise 0). The clean-up routines are entered by an always-taken branch and link, not `jal`. `v0` is the clean-up result on those paths and untouched otherwise.
